# Rung-3 acceptance, the borrow repair — E6⁗-A, E6⁗-WS, E6⁗-WS0, E-flip, E6-again′, E7⁗, E0‴

The record of whether the `&e` rule repaired on 2026-09-05 — a shared borrow
is exempt only where the borrowing CALL's product is provably dropped —
accuses falsely where the repaired shapes actually EXECUTE, and of whether the
arm that measures it can reach those shapes at all.

This is a **new slice, not a re-roll**. The 2026-09-05 E6‴ acceptance
(`docs/superpowers/acceptance/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-e6ppp.md`) was
measured once and stands as written: E6‴-A and E6‴-W **PASS**, 0 false
accusations of 14 lines under both readings, and its §5.1 recorded that
`--lib` reached 2 of 29 blast-radius arms. Nothing there is re-run, re-scoped
or re-classified by this document. The binding design is
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-borrow-repair-design.md`
(B1–B8), which adds to and changes nothing in
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-04-sensorium-rung3-err-flow-design.md` (R1–R16
— R15's "both readings and the guarded-arm count beside them" and R16's
residual list bind here unchanged); the plan is
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-borrow-repair.md`.

Two drivers are measured, and which is which is the point of the control. The
**HEAD driver** is the `cargo-sensorium` binary built `--release` from this
branch (`feat/rung3-borrow-repair`) by the runner at run time, with its commit
and sha256 recorded in §2; E6⁗-A and E6⁗-WS run under it. The **BASE driver**
is built `--release` from the pre-repair `main` at `d1b1b57` in a worktree
under `/mnt/extra`, and E6⁗-WS0 — the discrimination control — runs under it,
into its own from-scratch target and its own trace store. `cargo-sensorium`
has no `--version` flag, so each driver's identity is its worktree's HEAD plus
the binary's sha256, and the version string each arm reports is read after the
run out of the trace that arm wrote.

**§1 is byte-locked.** It is committed ALONE, after the transformer, converter
and corpus changes and their tests but before any E6⁗ number has been read,
and it is design §4's pre-registration with the five census numbers Tasks 0
and 1 measured on the clone before this section was written filled in. The
runner refuses to run if this section differs from the commit that locked it —
compared with `awk '/^## 1/,/^## 2/'`, the sha recorded in §2 — and refuses
outright while no lock sha is set. §1 is not amended: unlike E6‴'s there is
one sha and no dated note inside it. A completed measurement is never
re-rolled, and a miss is a STOP with its number.

## 1. Pre-registration

| Id | Question | Method | Endpoint | Derivation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E6⁗-A | Does the repaired rule accuse falsely on the arm rung 3 measured? | E6‴-A verbatim: `cargo sensorium test -p bloomery-daemon --lib` on the clone under the HEAD driver; `exceptions <run> --limit 100000`; every SWALLOWED line hand-adjudicated against the source under design R15's reading (log-and-continue is a TRUE swallow; a match guard's read is the BODY's disposition). | **0 false accusations**; both readings reported (amended = the gate; strictest pre-lock beside it); the guarded-arm count beside both. Reported: lines per disposition. **Predicted, no gate: 14 lines, the five source shapes of E6‴ §4.1** — none of E6‴'s fourteen involves a `&e` borrow, so B1 should move nothing here. | The same arm, the same protocol, comparable line for line with E6‴. |
| E6⁗-WS | Does it accuse falsely where the repaired shapes EXECUTE? | `cargo sensorium test --workspace` (no `--lib`) on the clone under the HEAD driver, from a target the prep build emptied; `exceptions` on EVERY recorded process (lib, bin, integration-test and doctest binaries); the union of SWALLOWED lines hand-adjudicated as above. | **0 false accusations** over the union; both readings; guarded count. Reported: processes recorded, per-process tally lines, walls, and of the flip set (E-flip) which arms EXECUTED (a HANDLED event whose `how` starts `arm_` at that site), named one by one — an arm never reached is not evidence. | E6‴ §5.1: `--lib` reaches 2 of 29 blast-radius arms; integration tests are where the rest live. |
| E6⁗-WS0 | Does the arm REACH the shape the repair is for? | The same command under the driver built `--release` from `d1b1b57` (the pre-repair `main`), into `/mnt/extra/sensorium-rung2/bloomery-target-control` (from scratch) and its own `SENSORIUM_DIR`; the same adjudication. | **≥ 1 false accusation at an arm E-flip lists** (predicted: `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:515`, reached by `api_v1_test.rs::oversized_prompt_gets_honest_400_not_truncation` and `…_has_the_full_openai_error_envelope`; `:396` is predicted NOT reached — no test found that posts an unknown model without an agent header — and is reported either way). **Not a merge gate**: a 0 here makes E6⁗-WS NOT DISCRIMINATING and the record says so (B5). | rigorous-experiments §1: an endpoint must be shown able to fail. |
| E-flip | Which arms did the repair move, exactly? | Two from-scratch `--workspace --no-run` builds of the clone — base driver into the control target, HEAD driver into the acceptance target — and a diff of their `kind: "arm"` manifest rows keyed `(file, line)` with the `how` each writes. | **`api_v1.rs:396` and `:515` read `arm_handled` BEFORE and `arm_ambiguous` AFTER; every changed row is `arm_handled → arm_ambiguous` (no other transition); the count of changed rows == the frozen census delta.** Frozen before the lock from the census binary on the clone: `arms_handled_before = 65`, `arms_handled_after = 54`, `arms_escaped_before = 121`, `arms_escaped_after = 132`, `arm_sites = 225`. | Settles CARRIED-DEBT's "different sets" item for this repair by taking the manifest diff rung 3 did not. |
| E6-again′ | Did the repair or the hop move a corpus verdict? | E6's protocol verbatim over every `corpus/rust/*` case with an `exceptions` question — now 20 questions: the 18 of E6‴ plus `err_borrowed_into_value` and `keep_first_error`. Fresh corpus target. | **For every case: printed SWALLOWED lines == the registered set (equality); every swallow case's set non-empty; every `dispositions:` line equal. Any extra or missing line = STOP.** | Parent §8 E6, unchanged. |
| E7⁗ | Did anything move a line or a column? | `rust/tests/mechanics.sh` on the probe workspace with the HEAD driver. | **0 failures, 0 differences** (47 checks as of E6‴; the count reported). | The splice is untouched; the number says so. |
| E0‴ | Does the reader stay usable on the widest trace yet? | `info <run>` and `diff <run> <run>` on the E6⁗-WS process with the most events, 60 s kill armed. | **Both under 60 s**; walls reported. | Parent §8 E0; the workspace arm is the largest volume recorded so far. |

**Lens for every endpoint**: dev profile; the clone at `e209ed9`; the HEAD
driver built `--release` by the runner (commit + sha256 + `built_from`
recorded); the base driver built `--release` from `d1b1b57` in a worktree
under `/mnt/extra` (commit + sha256 recorded); each arm's target from scratch
by its prep build (stated); `~/workspace/bloomery` untouched (HEAD/porcelain
before and after); loads at every arm's start; nothing gated on a wall. Budgets
derived from the plain run measured before this design (17 s from scratch,
948 tests, 105 binaries): 30 min per build, 60 min per instrumented run, as
kill ceilings only. Launched detached (`setsid nohup`), pid file + `.DONE`/
`.FAILED` marker, nothing read before the marker exists.

**Reported without a gate**: the per-process SWALLOWED counts and tallies of
both WS arms side by side; the walls of the plain run, the instrumented runs
and the builds; the number of doctest processes recorded; E1-style plain/call
wall on `--workspace` (the rung-2 addendum lens); the guarded-arm rows named.

## 2. Environment

Measured 2026-09-05T09:10:46-0500 → 2026-09-05T09:13:13-0500 by `rust/tests/acceptance_e6q.py`, launched detached; the raw facts it recorded are `results-e6q-raw.json` in the gitignored plan ledger, with every command's log beside it. §3 below is rendered from `2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-e6q.results.json`, which `acceptance_schema_e6q.assemble_e6q` derived from that raw file.

**§1 byte-lock.** The runner refuses to start unless the locked range is byte-identical to the commit that locked it — and refuses outright while no lock sha is set. The range is awk '/^## 1/,/^## 2/' PLUS the definition of every footnote §1 references — here §1 references no footnote (`footnotes_in_range` = none), so the extended range and `awk '/^## 1/,/^## 2/'` are the same bytes. Checked at `559e617`: 5327 bytes, sha256 `c911724b0c391c801e7d67883ba9f8a8b71833e92728201c13bbb941550e7c27` on both sides — identical: yes. §1 was committed ALONE and never amended: there is no second sha (`original_lock` = None).

**Log locations.** Every command's log is under `/home/brice/workspace/sensorium/.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-borrow-repair/acceptance-e6q/logs`, one subdirectory per phase (`built-from`, `prep-head`, `prep-base`, `arm-a`, `arm-ws`, `arm-ws0`, `e6-again`, `e7q`, `e0ppp`). Each prep build opens its own `logs_at` block inside itself, so its `cargo -v` log lands under that prep's directory and the two cannot overwrite each other: `/home/brice/workspace/sensorium/.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-borrow-repair/acceptance-e6q/logs/prep-head/prep/prep-workspace.log` and `/home/brice/workspace/sensorium/.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-borrow-repair/acceptance-e6q/logs/prep-base/prep/prep-workspace.log`.

1-minute load at each phase's start: prep (--workspace --no-run, from scratch) 0.56, prep (--workspace --no-run, from scratch) 2.6, E6⁗-A 5.62, E6⁗-WS 5.62, E6⁗-WS0 2.98, E6-again′ 2.14, E7⁗ 2.05, E0‴ 2.05.

**The runner's own driver build was load-bearing, and the record says so
rather than assuming it.** `built_from` reports the HEAD driver's sha256
*before* the build as `09def88613fd566acfad4d047731a0b9831493f3158d4a19661024345ecf28fc`
— byte-identical to the BASE driver — and *after* it as
`ef95dc4fef1b7015422d254f65a7d1ce05eaff55f8bbf59d2a5922b8b1da438e`, `rebuilt:
yes`, from repo HEAD `caf371a` with a clean porcelain. The binary sitting at
the HEAD driver's path when this run started was the PRE-repair one (built
07:07 by Task 0 into `rust-target-base`, and the same bytes at the HEAD path
from an earlier build at 04:01, before the transformer changed). Had the
runner not built the driver itself — the instrument change E6‴ §5.8 asked
for — E6⁗-A and E6⁗-WS would have run the control's binary and this document
would have reported three arms of one driver as two.

**The two drivers are separated by sha256 and by the trace's `tool_hash`, not
by `driver_version`.** All three arms' traces report `cargo-sensorium 0.3.0`:
design B6 moves `cargo-sensorium` 0.3.0 → 0.3.1 in the release task, which is
AFTER the lock and after this measurement, so the repaired binary still
carries the pre-repair version string. What does separate them is recorded on
both sides: the HEAD driver's sha256 `ef95dc4f…` against the BASE driver's
`09def886…`, and each arm's own trace `tool_hash` — `849b55140090fd90` for
E6⁗-A and E6⁗-WS, `50132e86f96576eb` for E6⁗-WS0. `traces_missing` is `[]` for
all three arms, so every process the record counts wrote a trace the reader
could open. §5.7 carries this as a residual of the runner's stated
expectation.

**Overall: PASS, and — for the first time in this line of records — the
control discriminated.** The repaired rule reads 782 SWALLOWED lines across
every binary a `--workspace` run builds, and not one of them is false. The
pre-repair driver, on the same command, the same clone, the same 144
processes, prints those same 782 plus exactly **30 more**, all at
`&e`-through-a-function arms, all of them false. The two arms' summed
dispositions say the same thing from the other side: `swallowed 812,
ambiguous 300` before, `swallowed 782, ambiguous 330` after — thirty lines
did not vanish, they changed class.

So the sentence design B5 licenses is the one this record may write:
**measured — the repair removed thirty false accusations at seven arms the
measuring arm reached.** What it does not say is that the flip set is fully
exercised: 4 of the 11 flipped arms were never executed by any arm of this
run (§4.5), and nothing here is evidence about them.

clone's source:

* **355 lines, 50 sites — a written sink with no binding** (`let _ = …;`,
  `.ok()`, `.unwrap_or…`): the error is discarded at the semicolon or
  replaced by a substitute, and nothing downstream can tell. The largest are
  the per-test `fresh_dir`/`sandbox`/`scratch` helpers' `let _ =
  fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);` followed by `create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap()`.
* **374 lines, 6 sites — a GUARDED arm whose body never reads `e`**:
  `http.rs:236` (303), `memory/store.rs:96` (41), `drift.rs:694` (14),
  `drift.rs:291` (8), `drift.rs:457` (4), `task/exec.rs:380` (4). §4.4's
  guarded column and §5.3.
* **31 lines, 31 sites — a test's assertion arm**: `Err(<Variant> { .. }) =>
  {}` beside `other => panic!(..)`. The pattern binds nothing; the expected
  failure is consumed by the `match` and the test passes.
* **22 lines, 4 sites — a non-test arm that binds nothing** and synthesises
  its value from something other than the error: `task_loop.rs:690` (18, the
  window ladder's retry), `exec_run.rs:308` (2), `http.rs:295` (1),
  `memory/store.rs:92` (1, `Err(_) => parse_errors += 1`).
* **0 lines — log-and-continue.** Not one of the 782 is an `Err(e) =>` arm
  whose body formats or logs `e` and carries on. §5.4 says why that is a fact
  about bloomery and not about the rule.

**0** of the 782 lines was unparsable by the collector; **0** resolved to a
sink outside the clone root; **0** landed at any of the 11 arms E-flip lists.

### 4.4 The counts, all three arms, both readings

§1 requires the count under the amended reading (the gate) and under the
strictest pre-lock reading, with the guarded-arm count beside both (design
B4, parent R15), "so a reader who rejects the amendment can re-derive the
verdict". Every number below is a sum over the rows above it — §4.1's rows
are one printed line each, §4.2's and §4.3's carry a `lines` column — and
nothing here is computed by the instrument.

| Arm | Reading | Count | Guarded arms (B4/R15) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E6⁗-A | **The amended reading** (the GATE): a log-and-continue arm is a TRUE swallow; a match guard's read is the BODY's disposition | **0 false accusations of 14** | **2 of 14** (§4.1 rows 8 and 10 — `memory/store.rs:96`) | **PASS** |
| E6⁗-A | **The strictest pre-lock reading**: every log-and-continue arm counted FALSE as well | **0 false accusations of 14** | **2 of 14** | **PASS** |
| E6⁗-WS | **The amended reading** (the GATE) | **0 false accusations of 782** | **374 of 782** (§4.2 rows for `http.rs:236`, `memory/store.rs:96`, `drift.rs:694`, `:291`, `:457`, `task/exec.rs:380`) | **PASS** |
| E6⁗-WS | **The strictest pre-lock reading** | **0 false accusations of 782** | **374 of 782** | **PASS** |
| E6⁗-WS0 | **The amended reading** | **30 false accusations of 812**, all 30 at arms E-flip lists | **374 of 812** | **DISCRIMINATING** — this arm is the control and gates nothing |
| E6⁗-WS0 | **The strictest pre-lock reading** | **30 false accusations of 812** | **374 of 812** | **DISCRIMINATING** |

The two readings agree in all three arms, and the record says why rather than
leaving it to be assumed: **not one of the 1 608 adjudicated lines is a
log-and-continue arm.** The class the amendment was written for still does
not occur anywhere this workspace's tests reach — now measured over 144
processes and every binary a `--workspace` build produces, not just the lib
ones. It is pinned by `corpus/rust/logged_arm`, which E6-again′ exercised and
passed. §5.4 says why that is a fact about bloomery rather than a general one.

The guarded column is where the two arms of this record differ from E6‴'s,
and it is much larger: **374** of E6⁗-WS's 782 lines are arms whose `Err`
binding is read only by a match guard, against 2 of 14 in E6‴. §5.3 states
what the letter-reading of §1's "merely observed" would do to that number and
why the gate is the ruled reading.

### 4.5 The discrimination verdict, and which flipped arms EXECUTED

§1's E6⁗-WS0 endpoint is "**≥ 1 false accusation at an arm E-flip lists**",
and design B5 says what each answer licenses. The answer is **30**, at
**7** of the 11 arms, and E6⁗-WS prints **0** at those same 7 under the
repaired driver.

| Flipped arm | executed by E6⁗-WS? | executed by E6⁗-WS0? | WS0 SWALLOWED lines there | WS SWALLOWED lines there | reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:396` (`chat_completions`) | **yes**, 7 HANDLED events, `how = arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 7 HANDLED events, `how = arm_handled` | **7** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0: `map_error(&e, None)`'s product is the status and body the function returns |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:515` (`chat_completions`) | **yes**, 5 events, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 5 events, `arm_handled` | **5** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0: the arm's tail is the reply |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:82` (`infer`) | **yes**, 11 events, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 11 events, `arm_handled` | **11** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0: `Err(e) => map_error(&e),` IS the return value |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:57` (`create_agent`) | **yes**, 3 events, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 3 events, `arm_handled` | **3** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0, same shape |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:156` (`delete_agent`) | **yes**, 2 events, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 2 events, `arm_handled` | **2** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0, same shape |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/models.rs:140` (`unblock`) | **yes**, 1 event, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 1 event, `arm_handled` | **1** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0, same shape |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/pager/paging.rs:673` (`Pager::restore_image`) | **yes**, 2 events, `arm_ambiguous` | **yes**, 2 events, `arm_handled` | **1** | 0 | **FALSE** under WS0: `substrate_msg(&e)` is bound to `failure`, tested, and embedded in the returned `PagerError::Substrate(..)` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:93` (`suspend`) | no | no | — | — | never reached; not evidence either way |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:104` (`resume`) | no | no | — | — | never reached |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/models.rs:25` (`unload`) | no | no | — | — | never reached |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/codec_probe/boot.rs:110` (`run_boot_codec_probe`) | no | no | — | — | never reached |

Two of the numbers in that table are deliberately not the same and the record
keeps them apart: `paging.rs:673` fired **twice** (two HANDLED events) and
printed **one** SWALLOWED line under the control. An arm executing and a
chain reaching a verdict at that arm are different events, and collapsing
them would inflate either column.

**`api_v1.rs:396` and `:515` — §1 names both, and the run answers both.** §1
predicted `:515` would be reached (by `api_v1_test.rs::oversized_prompt_*`)
and `:396` would NOT be ("no test found that posts an unknown model without
an agent header"), and required `:396` to be reported either way. **Both were
reached, `:396` more often than `:515`** — 7 HANDLED events against 5, and 7
false accusations against 5 under the control. The prediction about `:396` is
falsified by the run's own instrument; §5.1 records it as the one place where
§1's no-gate expectations did not hold.

The computed evidence the runner produced agrees with the hand reading
line-for-line: `lines_at_flipped_sites` is **30** for WS0 and **0** for WS,
with **0** unresolved sinks and **0** sinks outside the clone root on either
side. It is evidence, not the verdict — whether a line is a FALSE accusation
is the reading of the clone's source in §4.3, and that reading is what says
all 30 are.

**What this licenses, in design B5's own words:** "the record may say
*measured: the repair removed a false accusation the arm reached*". It did,
thirty times, at seven arms. E6⁗-WS is **DISCRIMINATING**.

### 4.6 E-flip — every arm the repair moved, named

Two from-scratch `--workspace --no-run` builds of the same clone, ten seconds
apart, differing only in which driver compiled them: the BASE driver into
`bloomery-target-control` (emptied: it was created empty for this run) and
the HEAD driver into `bloomery-target` (emptied: 5 706 522 060 bytes
removed). Each build compiled **154** units and declared **225** distinct
`kind: "arm"` manifest rows. **0** rows carried more than one `how`, so every
site has a single class on each side and no row was folded into a transition
it does not have.

| # | Changed row | qualname | before → after |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:57` | `create_agent` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 2 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:82` | `infer` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 3 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:93` | `suspend` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 4 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:104` | `resume` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 5 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/agents.rs:156` | `delete_agent` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 6 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/models.rs:25` | `unload` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 7 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_native/models.rs:140` | `unblock` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 8 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:396` | `chat_completions` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 9 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:515` | `chat_completions` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 10 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/codec_probe/boot.rs:110` | `run_boot_codec_probe` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |
| 11 | `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/pager/paging.rs:673` | `Pager::restore_image` | `arm_handled` → `arm_ambiguous` |

* **Transitions**: `{arm_handled → arm_ambiguous: 11}` and nothing else. §1's
  "no other transition" is met with **0** rows of any other kind.
* **`only_before` = 0, `only_after` = 0**: the two builds declared the same
  225 sites, so no row was gained or lost and the diff is over the same
  population on both sides.
* **`changed_count == 11 == 65 − 54`**, §1's frozen `arms_handled_before −
  arms_handled_after`, cited from §1 and never re-derived. The delta and the
  diff are two instruments (a census binary reading the tree at Tasks 0/1, a
  manifest diff at run time) that agree exactly.
* Nine of the eleven are `Err(e) => map_error(&e)` or `map_error(&e, ..)` —
  seven as a bare arm tail, two as the `let (status, value) = …` shape §1
  names. The other two are `codec_probe/boot.rs:110`
  (`return journal_degraded(pager, fixture_set_unparseable_reason(&e))`, a
  borrow nested inside another call's argument) and `pager/paging.rs:673`
  (`Err(e) => substrate_msg(&e),` as the tail of a `let failure = match …`).
  Every one is B1's "the borrowing CALL's product is not dropped" shape, and
  none is a `report(&e);` statement — no dropped-value call site moved.

This settles, for THIS repair, the CARRIED-DEBT item rung 3 left open as "the
census's 31 and the reviewer's 31 are not the same set": the flip set here is
not a total against a total, it is eleven named rows.

## 5. Gaps

### 5.1 The one place §1's ungated predictions did not hold: `api_v1.rs:396`

§1's E6⁗-WS0 row predicts by name that `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/api_v1.rs:515`
would be reached "by `api_v1_test.rs::oversized_prompt_gets_honest_400_not_truncation`
and `…_has_the_full_openai_error_envelope`", and that `:396` would NOT be —
"no test found that posts an unknown model without an agent header" — with
the instruction to report it either way.

**Both were reached, and `:396` was the busier of the two**: 7 HANDLED events
and 7 false accusations under the control, against 5 and 5 for `:515`
(§4.5). The prediction is falsified by the run's own instrument, and it is
worth saying exactly what kind of miss it is. It is a miss of a *search*, not
of a rule: whoever wrote §1 looked for a test that posts an unknown model
without an agent header and did not find one, and the workspace has one (or
several) all the same. The endpoint it sat under is unaffected — `:396` makes
the control *more* discriminating, not less — but the lesson is the one
rigorous-experiments keeps returning: **a "no test reaches this" claim
derived by reading is a hypothesis, and this run is the first arm that could
test it.** Nothing was re-scoped when the number came back the other way; the
prediction stands in §1 as written and this section records that it was
wrong.

Every other ungated prediction held: E6⁗-A printed the predicted **14** lines
over the predicted five source shapes, E7⁗'s column shift was the predicted
**6**, and the E-flip count matched §1's frozen delta exactly.

### 5.2 The shape of §4's tables is not E6‴'s, and the reason is volume

E6‴ adjudicated 14 lines as 14 rows. This record adjudicates **1 608** —
14 + 782 + 812 — and the tables in §4.2 and §4.3 are therefore **one row per
distinct sink site with the number of printed lines it accounts for**, not
one row per printed line. §4.1, at 14 lines, keeps E6‴'s per-line shape
unchanged so the two records are still comparable row for row on that arm.

This is a presentation choice and it is stated because it could hide
something. Two things make it safe here and both are checkable from what is
published: the adjudication is a reading of the clone's SOURCE, which cannot
differ between two lines that resolve to the same `(file, line)`; and §3
above already prints **all 1 608 lines individually**, numbered, each with
the sink the trace resolved for it, so any reader can map a printed line to
its row and re-derive every count in §4.4. The per-site counts sum to 14, 782
and 812 exactly — no line is unaccounted and none is counted twice.

What a per-line table would have added is nothing but repetition: 303 of
E6⁗-WS's rows would have read `http.rs:236` and 46 more `common/drift.rs:181`.

### 5.3 The guarded arms are now 374 of 782, and the letter-reading would STOP

E6‴'s §5.2 recorded that §1's "merely observed" clause admits a
letter-reading — "read by a `&self` predicate (`.is_err()`)" covers a match
guard's `e.kind()` — under which every guarded arm is a FALSE accusation. The
gate is the ruled reading (design R15: *a match guard's read is the BODY's
disposition*), and this record's verdicts rest on it.

In E6‴ that disagreement moved 2 lines of 14. **Here it would move 374 of
782**, and both endpoints would be a STOP:

| Site | lines (WS) | the arm |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/http.rs:236` | 303 | `Err(e) if is_shutdown_signal(&e) => return,` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/memory/store.rs:96` | 41 | `Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => { }` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/drift.rs:694` | 14 | `Err(e) if e.kind() == NotFound => { return ProfileRead { … } }` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/drift.rs:291` | 8 | `Err(e) if e.kind() == NotFound => { return Ok(Rotation::NothingToRotate { … }) }` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/drift.rs:457` | 4 | `Err(e) if e.kind() == NotFound => None,` |
| `crates/bloomery-daemon/src/task/exec.rs:380` | 4 | `Err(e) if e.kind() == NotFound => (String::new(), PreTouch::Absent),` |
| **total** | **374** | — |

E6⁗-A's 2 (`memory/store.rs:96`) and E6⁗-WS0's 374 are the same class. The
three reasons the ruled reading rests on are E6‴ §5.2's and are not repeated;
what this arm adds is scale — **the clause E6‴ could call a wording debt now
decides 48% of a headline**, and the wording debt on §1 should be paid before
rung 4 rather than inherited a third time. `http.rs:236` alone is 39% of this
arm's SWALLOWED volume, and it is the cleanest possible case for the ruled
reading: the guard identifies a deliberate `Server::unblock()` wake, the body
is a bare `return`, and no caller, log or value ever learns anything.

### 5.4 Three classes a reader may reasonably contest, all adjudicated TRUE

E6‴ §5.9 named one such row. This arm's volume surfaces three classes, and
each is TRUE under §1, R2 and R8 *and* is code whose author would reasonably
want the tool to stop mentioning. Naming them is not hedging the verdict; it
is the same distinction that record drew — the disposition is about where the
failure went, not about whether the code is right.

**(a) Test assertion arms — 31 lines at 31 sites.** `match g.check_write(&p)
{ Err(GrantViolation::PathOutsideRoots { .. }) => {} other => panic!(..) }`
and 30 more like it across `grant_path_test.rs`, `pager_timeshare_test.rs`,
`gguf_test.rs`. The `Err` is the outcome the test *asserts*, the pattern binds
nothing, and the arm's empty body drops it. The tool is right that the value
went nowhere; the author's answer is "of course — that is the assertion".

**(b) Arms that translate a payload-free failure into a synthesised value —
22 lines at 4 sites.** `Err(RunFailure::TimedOut) => Observation { outcome:
format!("ran {program} timed out"), … }` (`exec_run.rs:308`),
`Err(BodyTooLarge) => respond_json(request, 413, …)` (`http.rs:295`),
`Err(PagerError::PromptTooLarge { .. }) if … => { rung += 1; }`
(`task_loop.rs:690`, 18 lines — the window ladder's retry), and
`Err(_) => parse_errors += 1` (`memory/store.rs:92`). In each the variant
carries no data to escape, so nothing can, and the caller does nevertheless
learn *something* happened — a timeout in an `Observation`, a 413 on the
wire, a rung. **This is the boundary the borrow repair does not move**: B1 is
about the value channel, and a failure whose whole content is its
discriminant has no value channel to close. A reader who says "the 413 IS the
failure reaching the client" is describing a different question from the one
`exceptions` answers.

**(c) `drift.rs:694`'s re-worded absence — 14 lines.** The guarded `NotFound`
arm returns `profile: Err(format!("{}: no such file", path.display()))`. The
text is synthesised from `path`; the `io::Error` itself is dropped, so the
arm is a true swallow of the value — and yet a caller does receive a
statement that the read failed. It is (b) with a guard on top, and it is
listed separately because a reader scanning for "did anything downstream
learn?" will stop here first.

None of the three is a `&e`-through-a-function shape, so none is touched by
this slice's repair, and no rule was changed after these numbers were read. A
per-site `--allow` (or an in-source marker) is the shape that would settle all
three; it is CARRIED-DEBT, not a change to this record.

### 5.5 Four of the eleven flipped arms were never reached

`api_native/agents.rs:93` (`suspend`), `:104` (`resume`),
`api_native/models.rs:25` (`unload`) and `codec_probe/boot.rs:110`
(`run_boot_codec_probe`) fired **0** HANDLED events in every arm of this run,
under either driver. Nothing here is evidence about them in either direction:
the repair moved them in the manifests (§4.6) and no execution has ever
exercised them.

Three of the four are the same `Err(e) => map_error(&e),` tail as the seven
that were reached, which makes them low-risk by shape argument — and a shape
argument is exactly what the control exists to replace. `boot.rs:110` is the
one genuinely distinct shape in the flip set that nothing executed:
`return journal_degraded(pager, fixture_set_unparseable_reason(&e))`, a borrow
nested inside another call's argument. **The repaired rule's treatment of the
nested-argument shape is asserted and unit-tested, not measured on a running
tree.** That is a smaller residual than the one this slice opened with — where
NO shape was measured — but it is the same kind, and it should be named as
such rather than covered by the seven that did run.

### 5.6 What this run did not measure

* **Nothing was dropped except by design.** Every `{value, n, lens, dropped}`
  cell in `results.json` carries a value except **five**, each null with its
  reason and no cell anywhere carrying both a value and a dropped reason:
  the three arms' `false accusations` headlines (no program can adjudicate a
  SWALLOWED line against the clone's source, so the schema publishes the
  lines and refuses to publish a count it did not compute — the counts are
  §4.4); E6⁗-WS0's `discriminating` (decided by §4.5's hand reading, not by a
  line count); and E6⁗-A's "SWALLOWED lines the sweep added", which is a
  0-of-0 — that arm recorded one process, so its sweep read none and had
  nothing to add. That last cell is E6‴ §5.10(b)'s fix arriving as a default
  rather than as a correction.
* **The other rung-3 endpoints were not re-run.** §1 pre-registers seven and
  this run took exactly those seven. E1–E5, E2″, E3″ and the rest stand as
  the 2026-09-04 and 2026-09-05 records wrote them.
* **The census was not re-run.** §1 froze five numbers before the lock; this
  document cites them and E-flip's independent agreement (11 == 65 − 54) is
  corroboration, not a re-measurement. The sixth number the T0/T1 ledger
  carries (`arms_propagate` 39) is published beside them under its own label,
  never under §1's.
* **No wall was gated.** The E6⁗-WS instrumented run took 44.4 s and E6⁗-WS0
  43.4 s (against a 60-minute kill ceiling); each prep build ~10.1 s (against
  30 minutes). The plain-versus-instrumented comparison on `--workspace` that
  §1 lists under "reported without a gate" as the rung-2 addendum lens was
  **not taken** — no uninstrumented `--workspace` run was timed in this run,
  so that row is absent rather than zero.
* **Doctest processes were not counted separately.** §1 lists "the number of
  doctest processes recorded" as reported-without-a-gate. The runner records
  144 processes per WS arm and does not classify them by target kind, so this
  document reports the total and **not** the doctest split. It is a reporting
  hole in the instrument, found after the numbers were read and therefore
  recorded rather than repaired.

### 5.7 The two drivers' own version strings cannot tell them apart

`acceptance_e6q.py`'s module doc says the control arm's traces "must say
`cargo-sensorium 0.3.0` and the HEAD arms' the repaired version". All three
arms report `cargo-sensorium 0.3.0` (§2): design B6 moves the crate to 0.3.1
in the release task, which runs after this measurement, so the repaired
binary still carries the old string. The expectation was written before the
ordering was settled and this run falsifies it.

Nothing in §3 or §4 rests on it. Which driver ran which arm is established
three other ways, all recorded: the binary's sha256 (`ef95dc4f…` vs
`09def886…`, both pinned in §2 and unchanged across the run), each arm's own
trace `tool_hash` (`849b55140090fd90` vs `50132e86f96576eb`), and the
manifests the two prep builds wrote (`arm_handled` 54 vs 65 over the same 225
sites). The residual is that a reader who trusts `driver_version` alone gets
no signal; the fix belongs to the release task, which moves the string.

### 5.8 What was committed with this record, and why it is not an instrument change

Two files change alongside §2–§5, and neither moves a measured number:

* `rust/tests/acceptance_schema_e6q.py`'s `GUARDED_ARMS_E6Q` goes from
  `{None, None, None}` to `{2, 374, 374}` — the count §4.4 publishes by hand,
  restated in the cell where a reader compares the two readings, exactly as
  E6‴'s `GUARDED_ARMS` restates its §5.2. Filling it forced one adjacent
  correction: the cell's `dropped` list was hard-coded to "§5 … is written by
  the measurement task", which with a value present would have published a
  cell that is both measured and not-measured, against this schema's own
  stated rule. It is now `[]` when the arm ran and keeps its "the arm did not
  run" reason otherwise — the shape `acceptance_schema_e6ppp` already uses.
* `tests/test_acceptance_e6q.py`'s guarded-arm test pinned the pre-measurement
  state ("null until §5 is written"). That state is gone, so the test now pins
  the post-measurement invariant instead — the value, an empty `dropped`, the
  provenance, and still null for an arm that never ran. No test was added (the
  file is at its 800-line ceiling) and none was deleted.

`results-e6q-raw.json` was written once by the run and nothing since has
touched it; `2026-09-05-sensorium-rung3-e6q.results.json` is re-derivable from
it by `acceptance_e6q.py --assemble` at any time, which is what
`test_results_json_if_present_matches_the_committed_schema` checks now that
the file exists.

### 5.9 Residuals found by this run, recorded and not repaired

Post-lock, no code changed. These are the three things the run itself
surfaced, in the order a reader would meet them:

1. **§1's `:396` prediction was wrong** (§5.1) — a search result treated as a
   fact. No endpoint moved.
2. **The doctest-process split §1 asks for is not something the runner
   computes** (§5.6). The number is absent from this record; the instrument
   would need a per-process target-kind field to carry it.
3. **`driver_version` cannot separate the drivers this slice compares**
   (§5.7). Resolved by the release task's version bump, not by this record.

And the two the earlier tasks recorded, unchanged by this run: design B2's
side-channel blind spot (`self.record(&e);` still reads `arm_handled`,
`rust/HONESTY-BLIND-SPOTS.md` item 23 (d)), and E6‴ §5.3's nested
value-format macro inside a logging macro. Neither has any exposure on this
clone, and this run — 1 608 adjudicated lines over every binary a
`--workspace` build produces — could not have falsified either and does not.
